### [`RatFunc.Luroth.Φ_natDegree_eq_φ_natDegree`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/FieldTheory/RatFunc/Luroth.lean#L182-L185) — `mathlib-0077`: confirmed

- `(h : E ≠ ⊥)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The equation also holds when E = ⊥, because of junk values, so we can prove it by cases on `E = ⊥`. If E ≠ ⊥, the existing proof works unchanged. If E = ⊥, then X is transcendental over ⊥: ⊥ is algebraic over K, so if X were integral over ⊥ it would be integral over K by transitivity, which contradicts `transcendental_X`. Hence `φ ⊥ = minpoly ⊥ X = 0` (`minpoly.eq_zero`), and its natDegree is 0. This gives `(φ E).map _ = 0`. Then `Φ' E = integerNormalization _ 0 = 0` by `IsFractionRing.integerNormalization_eq_zero_iff` (K[X] is a domain). So `Φ E = primPart 0 = 1` by `Polynomial.primPart_zero`, since `primPart` is defined as `if p = 0 then 1 else …`. So `natDegree 1 = 0 = natDegree 0`, and the statement holds without `h`.
  - verifier: `h` is an explicit binder and is not used anywhere else in the statement, so the statement still elaborates without it. The case E ≠ ⊥ is the existing proof. Case E = ⊥: X is transcendental over K (`transcendental_X`), and ⊥ is algebraic over K, so by transitivity X is not integral over ⊥. Hence `φ ⊥ = minpoly ⊥ X = 0` (`minpoly.eq_zero`), whose natDegree is 0. The mapped polynomial `(φ ⊥).map _` is then 0, so `Φ' ⊥ = integerNormalization _ 0 = 0`. This follows from `IsFractionRing.integerNormalization_eq_zero_iff` (Localization/Integral.lean:97), or from the fact that the integer normalization of 0 is 0. I checked that `primPart` is defined as `if p = 0 then 1 else …` and that `primPart_zero : primPart 0 = 1` exists. So `Φ ⊥ = 1`, its natDegree is 0, and both sides equal 0. The statement therefore holds without `h`.

### [`Module.Basis.toMatrix_mulVec_repr`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Matrix/Basis.lean#L231-L236) — `mathlib-0083`: confirmed

- `[Finite ι']` — **confirmed**
  - judge: Nothing else in the statement mentions `[Finite ι']`. The product `b'.toMatrix b *ᵥ b.repr m` only needs `[Fintype ι]`, because `b'.toMatrix b : Matrix ι' ι R` and the sum in `mulVec` runs over `ι`. Both sides are functions `ι' → R`, and the identity holds entry by entry for any index type `ι'`. For each `i'`, the left side is `∑ i, b'.repr (b i) i' * b.repr m i` (by `Basis.toMatrix_apply`, which is `rfl`). The right side is `b'.repr m i'`. Rewrite `m` as `∑ i, b.repr m i • b i` using `Basis.sum_repr`, which needs only `[Fintype ι]` and is already used as `e.sum_repr` in this file. Then `map_sum`, `Finsupp.finset_sum_apply`, `map_smul`, `Finsupp.smul_apply` and `smul_eq_mul`/`mul_comm` turn the right side into the left side. No step uses finiteness of `ι'`. The degenerate cases (empty `ι`, trivial `R`) are covered by the same computation: the sums are empty or everything is zero. The current proof only needs `Finite ι'` because it goes through `LinearMap.toMatrix`. A direct proof (`ext i'`, then `conv_rhs => rw [← b.sum_repr m]`, then `simp [mulVec, dotProduct, Basis.toMatrix_apply, map_sum, mul_comm]`) works without it.
  - verifier: `[Fintype ι']` is already omitted, so `[Finite ι']` is the only finiteness assumption on ι', and nothing in the statement needs it. `Basis.toMatrix` (line 51) is defined for any index types: `b'.toMatrix b : Matrix ι' ι R`. `*ᵥ` needs only `[Fintype ι]`, which the theorem gets from `variable` because `b` mentions ι. Deleting `[Finite ι']` therefore leaves a statement that still elaborates. The identity is also still true. Entry by entry, the left side at `i'` is `∑ j : ι, b'.repr (b j) i' * b.repr m j`. This is exactly the left side of `Module.Basis.sum_repr_mul_repr` (Mathlib/LinearAlgebra/Basis/Defs.lean:641), taken with basis `b'` and with `b` as the finitely indexed basis. That lemma needs only `[Fintype ι]` and puts no finiteness condition on the outer basis's index type. It gives `b'.repr m i'`. So `ext i'; exact b'.sum_repr_mul_repr b m i'` (perhaps after unfolding `mulVec`/`dotProduct`), or the first reviewer's direct `sum_repr` computation, proves the statement without `Finite ι'`. Only the current proof, which goes through `LinearMap.toMatrix`, needs finiteness.

### [`MeasureTheory.projectiveFamilyContent_mono`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Constructions/ProjectiveFamilyContent.lean#L134-L137) — `mathlib-0116`: confirmed

- `(hs : s ∈ measurableCylinders α)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: `projectiveFamilyContent hP s` is definitionally `projectiveFamilyFun P s`: the lemma `projectiveFamilyContent_eq` proves this by `rfl`, because `IsSetRing.addContent_of_union` sets `toFun := m`. `projectiveFamilyFun P s` is defined as `if hs : s ∈ measurableCylinders α then P _ _ else 0`. The proof without `hs` splits on whether `s ∈ measurableCylinders α` (`by_cases hs : s ∈ measurableCylinders α`). If it is, the original proof `addContent_mono isSetSemiring_measurableCylinders hs ht hst` still works. If it is not, `rw [projectiveFamilyContent_eq, projectiveFamilyFun, dif_neg hs]` reduces the left side to `0`, and `0 ≤ _` holds in `ℝ≥0∞` by `zero_le _`. So the statement stays true and provable for every `s ⊆ t`. `ht` cannot be dropped the same way: if `t` is not a measurable cylinder, its content is `0`, while a measurable cylinder `s ⊆ t` can have positive content.
  - verifier: `hs` is an explicit binder of the theorem. Nothing else in the statement mentions it: `s` is still bound by the `variable` command and is used in `hst` and in the conclusion, so the statement elaborates without `hs`. The lemma `projectiveFamilyContent_eq` (line 122) states `projectiveFamilyContent hP s = projectiveFamilyFun P s` for every `s` and is proved by `rfl`. `projectiveFamilyFun` (line 71) is `if hs : s ∈ measurableCylinders α then P _ _ else 0`, using a classical decidability instance. So when `s` is not a measurable cylinder, `rw [projectiveFamilyContent_eq, projectiveFamilyFun, dif_neg hs]` turns the left side into `0`, and `zero_le _` in ℝ≥0∞ closes the goal. When `s` is a measurable cylinder, the original proof `addContent_mono isSetSemiring_measurableCylinders hs ht hst` still applies. So the theorem stays true and provable without `hs`. `ht` really is needed: for `t` outside the measurable cylinders the right side is 0. For example, take `s = univ`, a measurable cylinder whose content can be positive, and `t = univ`. Then `t` is a cylinder, so this shows nothing. Instead take `s = ∅`? That gives content 0, also no counterexample. The point is only that a positive-content cylinder `s` inside a non-cylinder `t` gives content > 0 on the left and 0 on the right. This does not affect the claim about `hs`.

### [`AdicCompletion.tensor_map_id_left_injective_of_injective`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/RingTheory/AdicCompletion/AsTensorProduct.lean#L367-L373) — `mathlib-0139`: confirmed

  - judge: For a Noetherian ring R, `AdicCompletion I R` is flat over R. The instance `AdicCompletion.flat_of_isNoetherian` at line 378 of the same file proves this. Flatness makes `lTensor (AdicCompletion I R) f` injective for every injective `f`, with no finiteness condition on M or N. The lemma for this is `Module.Flat.lTensor_preserves_injective_linearMap` in Mathlib/RingTheory/Flat/Basic.lean. As a function, `AlgebraTensorModule.map LinearMap.id f` is the same map as `f.lTensor _`; both agree on pure tensors.

The flatness instance is currently proved from this very lemma, so the order needs care, but there is no real circularity. The flatness proof only uses the lemma for `J.subtype : J → R` with J an ideal. Both `J` and `R` are finite R-modules when R is Noetherian, and both live in universe u. So the new proof goes as follows. First prove flatness inside the proof: use `Module.Flat.iff_lTensor_injective'`, then the current argument (`tensor_map_id_left_eq_map` and `map_injective`) with M := J and N := R. Then apply `lTensor_preserves_injective_linearMap` to `f` and `hf`. This never uses `Module.Finite R N`. The only remaining hypotheses needed are `[IsNoetherianRing R]` and `hf`. The same argument also removes `[Module.Finite R M]`, so the two can be deleted together.

Proof without it: `Module.Flat.iff_lTensor_injective'` (Flat/Tensor.lean:73) says flatness follows from injectivity of `lTensor (AdicCompletion I R) J.subtype` for every ideal J. For each ideal J, the existing argument (`tensor_map_id_left_eq_map` and `map_injective`) applies with M := J and N := R. Both live in `Type u` and both are `Module.Finite` because R is Noetherian. The function `AlgebraTensorModule.map LinearMap.id J.subtype` is the same function as `J.subtype.lTensor _`: both are additive and agree on pure tensors. This is the proof that the instance `flat_of_isNoetherian` (line 378) already uses, so `Module.Flat R (AdicCompletion I R)` holds. Next, `Module.Flat.lTensor_preserves_injective_linearMap` (Flat/Basic.lean:132) has no universe or finiteness condition on the source and target. With `f` and `hf` it gives injectivity of `f.lTensor (AdicCompletion I R)`, which is the same function as `AlgebraTensorModule.map LinearMap.id f`. Nothing in this uses finiteness of N. There is no circularity: flatness is first proved inside the proof from the finite case.
- `[Module.Finite R M]` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The argument is the same as for `[Module.Finite R N]`. Since R is Noetherian, `AdicCompletion I R` is flat over R. ### [`Convex.norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_fderiv_le`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Analysis/Calculus/MeanValue.lean#L498-L504) — `mathlib-0148`: confirmed

- `[IsRCLikeNormedField 𝕜]` — **confirmed**
  - judge: Nothing else in the statement needs this instance to elaborate: `fderiv 𝕜`, `DifferentiableAt 𝕜` and the operator norm only use `NontriviallyNormedField 𝕜`, `NormedSpace 𝕜 E` and `NormedSpace 𝕜 G`. The current proof uses it only through `norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_hasFDerivWithin_le`, which builds an `RCLike 𝕜` instance and restricts scalars to give `G` a real structure. The mean value inequality holds for any nontrivially normed field 𝕜 by a direct argument. Let v = y - x and g(t) = f(x + t•v) for t ∈ [0,1].

(1) The segment lies in s because `hs.mapsTo_lineMap` is used with `xs` and `ys`. At each point of s, f is differentiable over 𝕜, so it is continuous there. Hence g is continuous on [0,1].

(2) Fix t ∈ [0,1) and put L = fderiv 𝕜 f (g t). For z > t, take w = (z-t)•v (real scalar multiplication), so w → 0 in E as z → t. `HasFDerivAt` over 𝕜 gives ‖f(g t + w) - f(g t) - L w‖ = o(‖w‖). The operator-norm bound `le_opNorm`, which holds over any normed field, gives ‖L w‖ ≤ ‖L‖‖w‖ ≤ C (z-t)‖v‖. So for every ε > 0, eventually as z → t⁺ we have ‖g z - g t‖ ≤ (C‖v‖ + ε)(z-t). By the triangle inequality, the slope of t ↦ ‖g t - g 0‖ is therefore eventually < C‖v‖ + ε.

(3) Apply Mathlib's `image_norm_le_of_liminf_right_slope_norm_lt_deriv_boundary` (MeanValue.lean, line 224). It only needs `[NormedAddCommGroup E]`, with no scalar field. Use it with the function t ↦ g t - g 0, the slope bound f' = C‖v‖ + ε, and the boundary B(t) = (C‖v‖ + 2ε)t with B' = C‖v‖ + 2ε. This gives ‖f y - f x‖ ≤ C‖v‖ + 2ε for every ε > 0, hence ‖f y - f x‖ ≤ C‖y - x‖.

The argument never uses an RCLike structure on 𝕜 or a real structure on G. Degenerate cases are fine: if x = y both sides are 0, and the bound on `fderiv` stays valid under its junk value because `hf` gives differentiability on s. So the theorem stays true and provable after deleting `[IsRCLikeNormedField 𝕜]` (via `omit [IsRCLikeNormedField 𝕜] in`).
  - verifier: It is an argument. It is instance-implicit, and 𝕜 is an argument because it appears in `fderiv 𝕜` and `DifferentiableAt 𝕜`. Nothing in the statement needs it to elaborate: `DifferentiableAt 𝕜`, `fderiv 𝕜` and the operator norm on `E →L[𝕜] G` only need `NontriviallyNormedField 𝕜` and the normed-space instances. The current proof uses it only to build `RCLike 𝕜` and a real structure on G inside `norm_image_sub_le_of_norm_hasFDerivWithin_le` (line 426). The reviewer's direct argument works over any nontrivially normed field. Let g(t) = f(x + t•(y−x)). By `hs.mapsTo_lineMap`, g maps [0,1] into s. f is continuous at each point of s (`DifferentiableAt.continuousAt`), so g is continuous on [0,1]. Fix t ∈ [0,1) and let L = fderiv 𝕜 f (g t). As z → t⁺, w = (z−t)•v → 0 in E. `HasFDerivAt` gives ‖f(g t + w) − f(g t) − L w‖ = o(‖w‖), and `le_opNorm` gives ‖L w‖ ≤ C(z−t)‖v‖. So for any r > C‖v‖, eventually (hence frequently, since 𝓝[>] t is NeBot) slope(‖g · − g 0‖) t z ≤ ‖g z − g t‖/(z−t) < r. `image_norm_le_of_liminf_right_slope_norm_lt_deriv_boundary` (line 224) exists and only needs `[NormedAddCommGroup E]`. Apply it with f' = C‖v‖ and B(t) = (C‖v‖+ε)t, which is continuous and has derivative C‖v‖+ε > f'. This gives ‖f y − f x‖ ≤ C‖v‖ + ε for every ε > 0, which proves the claim. No RCLike structure is used. A sanity check agrees: a real normed space E that is also a compatible 𝕜-normed space forces 𝕜 to be archimedean, or E to be trivial. Even in exotic cases such as 𝕜 = ℚ with G a ℚ-normed space without a real structure, the direct argument above still applies.

### [`continuous_parametric_integral_of_continuous`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/MeasureTheory/Integral/Bochner/Set.lean#L1121-L1139) — `mathlib-0190`: confirmed

- `[FirstCountableTopology X]` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The current proof needs first countability only for `continuousAt_of_dominated` (Bochner/Basic.lean). That lemma lives under `variable [FirstCountableTopology X]` because dominated convergence needs a countably generated filter. A uniform estimate replaces it, as in the interval version `intervalIntegral.continuous_parametric_primitive_of_continuous` (DominatedConvergence.lean), which assumes only `[TopologicalSpace X]` on X.

Argument: fix x₀ and ε > 0. `IsCompact.mem_uniformity_of_prod hs hf.continuousOn (mem_univ x₀) (dist_mem_uniformity δpos)` (HeineCantor.lean) gives v ∈ 𝓝 x₀ such that ‖f x y - f x₀ y‖ < δ for all x ∈ v and y ∈ s. This uses only compactness of s, not first countability or local compactness of X.

Integrability: each f x is continuous, so it is AEStronglyMeasurable for μ.restrict s by `Continuous.aestronglyMeasurable`, using `SecondCountableTopologyEither Y E` and `OpensMeasurableSpace Y`. It is bounded on s because s is compact. The set {t | ‖f x t‖ ≤ M} is closed, hence measurable, so `ae_restrict_iff` turns the pointwise bound on s into an a.e. bound, exactly as in the current proof. Also μ s < ∞ by `hs.measure_ne_top`.

Conclusion: by `integral_sub`, the difference of the integrals is ∫ y in s, (f x y - f x₀ y) ∂μ. By `norm_setIntegral_le_of_norm_le_const` (Bochner/Set.lean, which needs only μ s < ∞), its norm is at most δ · μ.real s < ε for small δ. So the map is continuous at every x₀.

Degenerate cases: if s is empty or μ s = 0, both integrals are 0 and the bound still holds. Both `[FirstCountableTopology X]` and `[LocallyCompactSpace X]` can be dropped together.
  - verifier: The instance is a binder of the theorem and nothing else in the statement mentions it. Its only use is `continuousAt_of_dominated`, which sits under `variable [FirstCountableTopology X]`. The replacement proof is a uniform estimate, and the lemmas it cites exist as claimed.

1. `IsCompact.mem_uniformity_of_prod` (HeineCantor.lean:119) needs only `[TopologicalSpace α] [TopologicalSpace β] [UniformSpace E]`. Apply it with its set `s := univ` in X, `k := s`, `hf.continuousOn` and `dist_mem_uniformity`. Since `𝓝[univ] x₀ = 𝓝 x₀`, this gives v ∈ 𝓝 x₀ with dist (f x y) (f x₀ y) < δ for all x ∈ v and y ∈ s.
2. `norm_setIntegral_le_of_norm_le_const` (Bochner/Set.lean:609) needs only `μ s < ∞` and a pointwise bound on s, not even measurability. `μ s < ∞` follows from `hs.measure_ne_top`, using `IsLocallyFiniteMeasure`.
3. `integral_sub` needs integrability of f x and f x₀ on s. Each is continuous, so it is AEStronglyMeasurable using `SecondCountableTopologyEither Y E` and `OpensMeasurableSpace Y`. Each is bounded on the compact s, and s has finite measure.

Together these give ‖∫_s f x - ∫_s f x₀‖ ≤ δ · μ.real s, which can be made smaller than any ε. If E is not complete, both integrals are 0 by definition and the map is trivially continuous. The argument uses neither first countability nor local compactness of X.
- `[LocallyCompactSpace X]` — **confirmed**
  - judge: Local compactness is used only to get a compact neighbourhood U of x₀, so that ‖f‖ is bounded by some M on U ×ˢ s, which gives the dominating constant. `IsCompact.mem_uniformity_of_prod hs hf.continuousOn (mem_univ x₀) (dist_mem_uniformity one_pos)` (HeineCantor.lean) gives instead a neighbourhood v of x₀ with ‖f x y - f x₀ y‖ < 1 for x ∈ v and y ∈ s. Since {x₀} ×ˢ s is compact, ‖f x₀ ·‖ ≤ M₀ on s, so ‖f x y‖ ≤ M₀ + 1 on v ×ˢ s.

The rest of the proof is unchanged: `continuousAt_of_dominated` with the constant bound M₀ + 1, which is integrable on s by `integrableOn_const hs.measure_ne_top`, with the a.e. bound obtained through `ae_restrict_iff` and the closed set {t | ‖f x t‖ ≤ M₀ + 1}. Alternatively, the uniform-estimate proof described for `[FirstCountableTopology X]` needs neither instance. Each instance can be deleted alone, and both can be deleted together.
  - verifier: The instance is a binder of the theorem and nothing else in the statement mentions it. Its only use is `exists_compact_mem_nhds`, which provides the dominating constant.

Alternative bound: `IsCompact.mem_uniformity_of_prod hs hf.continuousOn (mem_univ x₀) (dist_mem_uniformity one_pos)` gives a neighbourhood v of x₀ with dist (f x y) (f x₀ y) < 1 on v × s. The bound M₀ comes from compactness of the image of s under the continuous map `f x₀`. Hence ‖f x y‖ ≤ M₀ + 1 on v × s.

The rest of the current proof goes through unchanged with constant bound M₀ + 1:
- `continuousAt_of_dominated` (Bochner/Basic.lean:456) takes eventual measurability, an eventual a.e. bound, an integrable bound and a.e. continuity. With first countability kept, these are supplied as before.

### [`add_tsub_add_le_tsub_right`](https://github.com/leanprover-community/mathlib4/blob/e3e75e86143b43849829fdcd44a39a6b0fe35234/Mathlib/Algebra/Order/Sub/Defs.lean#L160-L162) — `mathlib-0260`: confirmed

- `[AddLeftMono α]` — **confirmed**
  - judge: `[Preorder α] [AddCommSemigroup α] [Sub α] [OrderedSub α]` already make addition monotone, so `AddLeftMono` adds nothing.

**Monotonicity from `OrderedSub`.** Suppose x ≤ y and take any c.
1. `add_tsub_le_right` (Defs.lean line 68, which needs only `OrderedSub`) gives x + c - c ≤ x.
2. Then x + c - c ≤ x ≤ y.
3. `tsub_le_iff_right` (a - b ≤ c ↔ a ≤ c + b, which is the definition of `OrderedSub`) turns this into x + c ≤ y + c.

With commutativity this gives monotonicity on both sides, which is all `AddLeftMono` provides.

**Proof of the theorem without it.**
1. By `tsub_le_iff_left` (line 91, no monotonicity needed), the goal a + c - (b + c) ≤ a - b is equivalent to a + c ≤ (b + c) + (a - b).
2. `le_tsub_add` (line 71, no monotonicity needed) gives a ≤ (a - b) + b.
3. By the monotonicity argument above, a + c ≤ ((a - b) + b) + c.
4. By associativity and commutativity, ((a - b) + b) + c = (b + c) + (a - b), which closes the goal.

The argument does not depend on any special case: empty types or a trivial order cause no problem.
  - verifier: The instance comes from `variable [AddLeftMono α]` in section Cov. The theorem includes it because it is instance-implicit and its type mentions only α, but nothing in the statement `a + c - (b + c) ≤ a - b` needs it to elaborate. The argument that `OrderedSub` gives monotonicity checks out. `add_tsub_le_right` (line 68, needs only Preorder/Add/Sub/OrderedSub) gives x + c - c ≤ x. If x ≤ y, transitivity gives x + c - c ≤ y. Then `tsub_le_iff_right` (a - b ≤ c ↔ a ≤ c + b, the field of `OrderedSub`) turns this into x + c ≤ y + c. Proof without the instance: by `tsub_le_iff_left` (line 91, needs no monotonicity) the goal becomes a + c ≤ b + c + (a - b). `le_add_tsub` (line 93) gives a ≤ b + (a - b). Monotonicity as above gives a + c ≤ b + (a - b) + c. That equals b + c + (a - b) by `add_right_comm`. Every lemma used is stated before `section Cov` without `AddLeftMono`, and the argument has no gap, so the hypothesis can be deleted.

So the case N = 0 comes down to showing `(Gamma0 0).index = 0`, i.e. that Γ₀(0) has infinite index. Γ₀(0) is the group of upper-triangular matrices, and its index is indeed infinite. A short proof using lemmas that already exist: for every prime p, `Gamma0 0 ≤ Gamma0 p`, because by `mem_Gamma0_iff_dvd` membership means `(0:ℤ) ∣ γ 1 0`, so `γ 1 0 = 0`, which p divides. By Mathlib's `Subgroup.index_dvd_of_le`, `(Gamma0 p).index ∣ (Gamma0 0).index`. By `Gamma0_prime_power_index p hp 1` (or `Gamma0_prime_index`), `(Gamma0 p).index = p + 1`. If m := (Gamma0 0).index were nonzero, take a prime p ≥ m (`Nat.exists_infinite_primes`). Then p + 1 ∣ m with m > 0 gives p + 1 ≤ m ≤ p, a contradiction. So the index is 0 and both sides are 0. For N ≠ 0 the existing proof is unchanged. So the theorem stays true and provable without `hN`, by first splitting on whether N = 0.

Take a prime p ≥ m (by `Nat.exists_infinite_primes`). Then p + 1 ∣ m with m > 0 forces p + 1 ≤ m ≤ p, a contradiction. So m = 0, and both sides equal 0. For N ≠ 0 the existing proof still works, so splitting on N = 0 proves the theorem without `hN`.

Case 2: f is not differentiable at x. Then `mfderiv_zero_of_not_mdifferentiableAt` (Mathlib MFDeriv/Basic.lean:523) gives mfderiv f x = 0, so the left side is ω (f x) (fun _ ↦ 0). Suppose g := f ∘ (extChartAt I α).symm were MDifferentiableWithinAt (range I) z.

- `mdifferentiableAt_extChartAt` (Atlas.lean:345, which uses the `[IsManifold I 1 M]` still in scope) makes extChartAt I α MDifferentiableAt x.
- extChartAt I α x = z by right_inv, and extChartAt maps every point into range I.

Hence `mfderivWithin_zero_of_not_mdifferentiableWithinAt` (Basic.lean:519) gives mfderivWithin g = 0, and the right side is also ω (f x) (fun _ ↦ 0).

The two sides agree in both cases. hf appears nowhere else in the statement, so deleting it leaves a statement that elaborates. All cited lemmas exist with the stated hypotheses.

The equivalence still holds over any commutative ring if we work straight from the definition `IsParabolic g := ∃ a : SL(2, R), ↑a = g ∧ (a : GL (Fin 2) R).IsParabolic`.

Step 1. For every `a : SL(2, R)`, `(a⁻¹ : GL).IsParabolic ↔ (a : GL).IsParabolic`. Mathlib defines `Matrix.IsParabolic m` as `m ∉ Set.range (scalar _) ∧ m.discr = 0`, and `discr_fin_two` gives `discr = trace ^ 2 - 4 * det`. In SL(2, R) the inverse is the adjugate `!![d, -b; -c, a]`. It has the same trace and determinant as `a`, and it is scalar exactly when `b = 0`, `c = 0` and `a = d`, which is exactly when `a` is scalar. This is the calculation the existing proof does with `simp` after rewriting with `isParabolic_mk_iff`: the local `hscalar`, `adjugate_fin_two`, `trace_fin_two` and `det_fin_two` are all commutative-ring facts, and none of them needs `NoZeroDivisors`.

Step 2. If `IsParabolic g⁻¹`, choose `a` with `↑a = g⁻¹` and `a` parabolic. Then `↑a⁻¹ = g` (by `QuotientGroup.mk_inv` and `inv_inv`), and `a⁻¹` is parabolic by Step 1, so `IsParabolic g`. The converse is the same argument applied to `g⁻¹`, using `inv_inv`.

Degenerate case: if `R` is the trivial ring, every matrix is scalar, so both sides are false and the equivalence holds. The statement is therefore true without the instance, and it can be removed with `omit [NoZeroDivisors R] in`.

In SL(2, R) the inverse is the adjugate `!![d, -b; -c, a]`. It has trace `d + a` and determinant `da - bc`, the same as `a`, so the discriminants agree. It is scalar iff `-b = 0`, `-c = 0` and `d = a`, which is the same as `a` being scalar. So `(a⁻¹ : GL).IsParabolic ↔ (a : GL).IsParabolic` for every `a : SL(2, R)` over any commutative ring. Then use the existential definition directly. If `IsParabolic g⁻¹` is witnessed by `a`, with `↑a = g⁻¹` and `a` parabolic, then `↑a⁻¹ = (g⁻¹)⁻¹ = g` and `a⁻¹` is parabolic, so `IsParabolic g`. ### [`TauCeti.IsFiniteType.exists_chain_of_reachable`](https://github.com/TauCetiProject/TauCeti/blob/e8b7b600c36c975615413059bc967ec218cfefe0/TauCeti/LinearAlgebra/RootSystem/FiniteType/Diagram.lean#L154-L185) — `tauceti-0149`: confirmed

- `(h : IsFiniteType A)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: Without `h` the statement still holds for every integer matrix. Adjacency in `diagramGraph A` means `i ≠ j ∧ A i j ≠ 0 ∧ A j i ≠ 0` (`diagramGraph_adj`). The conclusion only involves forward entries along the chain: `A (w i) (w (i+1)) ≠ 0`, and `A (w i) (w j) = 0` for `i+1 < j ≤ n`. Backward entries never appear.

Proof without `h`. Call a pair `(n, w)` a chain if `w 0 = u`, `w n = v` and `A (w i) (w (i+1)) ≠ 0` for all `i < n`. A chain exists: take any walk `q` from `u` to `v` given by `huv`. Then `(q.length, q.getVert)` is a chain, because `q.adj_getVert_succ` together with `diagramGraph_adj` gives `A (q.getVert i) (q.getVert (i+1)) ≠ 0`; no symmetry is needed. Let `n` be the least length of a chain (`Nat.find`), with witness `w`.

(1) Injectivity on `{i | i ≤ n}`: if `w a = w b` with `a < b ≤ n`, drop the segment from `a+1` to `b`. This gives a chain of length `n - (b - a) < n`, contradicting minimality.

(2) No chords: if `A (w i) (w j) ≠ 0` with `i+1 < j ≤ n`, the sequence `w 0, …, w i, w j, …, w n` is a chain of length `n - (j - i) + 1 < n`, again a contradiction.

Degenerate cases: when `u = v` the minimal chain has `n = 0` and the conditions hold vacuously. Diagonal entries never occur in the chord condition, since `i+1 < j` forces `i ≠ j`.

The current proof uses `h` only to turn adjacency into a single nonzero entry (`diagramGraph_adj_iff`) and for acyclicity. Neither is needed, because the minimal-chain argument above produces the conclusion directly.
  - verifier: `diagramGraph A` is `SimpleGraph.fromRel fun i j ↦ A i j ≠ 0 ∧ A j i ≠ 0`, and `diagramGraph_adj` (lines 85–88) proves this lemma for any integer matrix, without `IsFiniteType`. So every walk given by `huv` has `A (w i) (w (i+1)) ≠ 0` at each step, with no symmetry assumption needed. `h` appears nowhere else in the statement. The conclusion only asks for a shortest directed path in the relation `A x y ≠ 0`, and that exists for any matrix. Take a chain of least length `n` (by `Nat.find`; a chain exists because of the walk). If `w a = w b` with `a < b ≤ n`, define `w' k = w k` for `k ≤ a` and `w' k = w (k + (b - a))` for `k > a`. This is a chain of length `n - (b - a)`: the step at `a` is `A (w b) (w (b+1)) ≠ 0`, and when `b = n` the endpoint is `w a = v`. That contradicts minimality, so `w` is injective on `{i | i ≤ n}`. If `A (w i) (w j) ≠ 0` with `i + 1 < j ≤ n`, skipping from `w i` to `w j` gives a chain of length `i + 1 + (n - j) < n`, again a contradiction. The case `u = v` gives `n = 0`, where everything holds vacuously. The statement still elaborates without `h`, it is true, and the proof sketch has no gap, so `h` is unnecessary (the current proof uses it only through `diagramGraph_adj_iff` and acyclicity).

Adding `have hd : 0 < d := by linarith` at the start of the proof restores it, and the rest of the proof is unchanged.
- `(hb : 0 ≤ b)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The statement stays true for b < 0, as long as d > 0. Let s = √(d²−4b²) and r = 2b/(d+s); note d+s > 0.
- If d² < 4b²: b·t² − d·t + b has a negative leading coefficient and a negative discriminant, so it is always < 0. Then `hquad` (which says it is ≥ 0) cannot hold, and the statement is vacuous.
- If d² ≥ 4b²: we still have s² = d²−4b², and the proof's `hroot` (b(1+r²) = d·r) is derived without `hb`. Suppose t > r. The existing factorisation is b(1+t²) − d·t = (t−r)(b(t+r) − d). Since r = (d−s)/(2b), we have 2br = d−s. As b < 0 and t+r > 2r, b(t+r) − d < 2br − d = −s ≤ 0. The product is then negative, which contradicts `hquad`.
So the proof only needs a case split on the sign of b; the case b ≥ 0 is the existing proof (`hb` is used only in `hcoef`).
  - verifier: I checked the math myself. The case b ≥ 0 is the existing proof. Take b < 0, with d > 0 still assumed; `hsmall` then holds automatically. The hypothesis `hquad` says b t² − d t + b ≥ 0. (a) If d² < 4b²: the quadratic has a negative leading coefficient and a negative discriminant, so it is < 0 for every t. Then `hquad` is false and the statement holds vacuously. (b) If d² ≥ 4b²: let s = √(d²−4b²) ≥ 0, so d + s > 0. Let r = 2b/(d+s); then r = (d−s)/(2b), the larger root. The existing `hroot` step (b(1+r²) = d r) needs only s² = d²−4b² and d+s ≠ 0, not `hb`. The factorisation b(1+t²) − d t = (b(1+r²) − d r) + (t−r)(b(t+r) − d) is a ring identity. If t > r, then t + r > 2r, and since b < 0 we get b(t+r) < 2br = d − s. So b(t+r) − d < −s ≤ 0, the product (t−r)(b(t+r)−d) is negative, and `hquad` fails. Hence t ≤ r. A case split on the sign of b gives a valid proof.
- `(hsmall : 2 * b < d)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: Given `hb`, `hd` and `ht1`, the case d ≤ 2b also satisfies the conclusion. Then b > 0 and d² − 4b² ≤ 0, so Real.sqrt of it is 0 (Mathlib's junk value for negative arguments; at d = 2b it is √0 = 0). The right-hand side becomes 2b/d ≥ 1 > t by `ht1`. The proof splits on 2b < d: in that case use the existing argument; otherwise rewrite the square root to 0 (Real.sqrt_eq_zero'.2) and conclude from (one_le_div hd).2 and `ht1`. `hsmall` can also be dropped together with `hb`, since b < 0 < d makes it automatic; it cannot be dropped together with `hd`.

Since d ≤ 2b and d > 0, 2b/d ≥ 1 > t by `ht1`, so the conclusion holds. `hsmall` is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement.

### [`RS.tensorPow_id_ne_zero`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/RegtsSevenster/RS/Classical/Deligne/SuperEmbed/Standard.lean#L336-L359) — `lean-pool-0048`: confirmed

- `[MonoidalPreadditive A]` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The proof uses `MonoidalPreadditive` at exactly one point: `MonoidalPreadditive.zero_whiskerRight`, to get from `𝟙 (T_k) = 0` to `𝟙 (T_k ⊗ U) = 0`, where `T_k = tensorPow A U k`. Without that instance, the hypothesis `hUU : U ⊗ U ≅ 𝟙_ A` already gives the same step.

Let F be the functor `- ⊗ U` (Mathlib's `tensorRight U`). The composite `(α_ X U U) ≪≫ whiskerLeftIso X hUU ≪≫ ρ_ X` is an isomorphism `(X ⊗ U) ⊗ U ≅ X`, and it is natural in X because the associator, `whiskerLeftIso` and the right unitor are all natural. So F ⋙ F ≅ 𝟭 A. Feeding F, F and this isomorphism to `Equivalence.mk` makes F part of an equivalence, so F is full.

Mathlib's instance `Functor.preservesZeroMorphisms_of_full` (Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Limits/Preserves/Shapes/Zero.lean) says that a full functor preserves zero morphisms. It needs only `HasZeroMorphisms`, which `Preadditive` supplies. Therefore `𝟙 (T_k ⊗ U) = F.map (𝟙 T_k) = F.map 0 = 0`. This replaces the `zero_whiskerRight` rewrite. Equivalently, `Functor.map_isZero` shows that F sends the zero object T_k to a zero object.

The rest of the proof stays as it is: the base case uses only `hone` and `IsZero.iff_id_eq_zero`, and `id_zero_transfer` uses only `Preadditive`. Degenerate cases: with `hone` still in place, k = 0 is unchanged, and the argument above holds for every object T_k, so the statement is still true for all k.

Step 2: the statement still elaborates without it. `tensorPow` (RS/Definitions.lean:2406) needs only `Category` and `MonoidalCategory`, the `0` morphism comes from `Preadditive`, and `IsZero` and `𝟙_ A` need nothing more. Step 5: the proof uses the instance only once, in the rewrite `MonoidalPreadditive.zero_whiskerRight`. The helper `id_zero_transfer` (line 329) takes only `[Category] [Preadditive]`. The proposed replacement works. Let F = tensorRight U. The components `α_ X U U ≪≫ whiskerLeftIso X hUU ≪≫ ρ_ X : (X ⊗ U) ⊗ U ≅ X` are natural in X, by naturality of the associator, whisker exchange and naturality of the right unitor. This gives F ⋙ F ≅ 𝟭 A. `Equivalence.mk F F η ε` (Mathlib/CategoryTheory/Equivalence.lean:340) takes exactly `η : 𝟭 ≅ F ⋙ G` and `ε : G ⋙ F ≅ 𝟭`, so we can pass the symmetric iso and the iso. Hence F is an equivalence, and so it is full. `Functor.preservesZeroMorphisms_of_full` (Limits/Preserves/Shapes/Zero.lean:97) needs only `HasZeroMorphisms` and `[Full F]`. So `𝟙 (T ⊗ U) = F.map (𝟙 T) = F.map 0 = 0`. The rest of the induction is unchanged. As a cross-check, F is an autoequivalence, so it sends the zero object T to a zero object; the statement is therefore true without the instance. The hypothesis can be deleted.

### [`RS.kernelSubobject_tensorHom_le`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/RegtsSevenster/RS/Classical/Deligne/KernelPow.lean#L729-L761) — `lean-pool-0091`: confirmed

The proof only uses it through `exists_kernel_ι_whiskerRight/Left`, where `PreservesKernel.iso (tensorRight W) p` needs `(tensorRight W).PreservesZeroMorphisms`. In a rigid category that already holds. The library's TensorExact.lean builds `tensorRightAdjunction X Xᘁ` and `tensorLeftAdjunction (ᘁX) X`, so `tensorRight X` and `tensorLeft X` are left adjoints. Mathlib's `preservesZeroMorphisms_of_isLeftAdjoint` (Limits/Preserves/Shapes/Zero.lean) then gives the instance; `preservesZeroMorphisms_of_preserves_initial_object` with the preserves-colimits instances works too. No degenerate case is affected, since the conclusion is an inequality of subobjects that never mentions additivity of the tensor product.

In the proof, `MonoidalPreadditive` is used only through `kernelSubobject_whiskerRight_le` and `factors_kernel_ι_whiskerLeft`. Both rest on `exists_kernel_ι_whisker{Right,Left}`, where `PreservesKernel.iso (tensorRight W) p` needs `(tensorRight W).PreservesZeroMorphisms`. Without `MonoidalPreadditive`, that instance still follows from rigidity. TensorExact.lean builds the adjunctions `tensorRightAdjunction X Xᘁ` and `tensorLeftAdjunction (ᘁX) X`, so tensoring on either side is a left adjoint. Mathlib's `preservesZeroMorphisms_of_isLeftAdjoint` (Limits/Preserves/Shapes/Zero.lean) then gives the instance, and it only needs zero morphisms on both sides, which `Abelian` provides. The kernel-preservation instances (`tensorRight_preservesLimits`/`tensorLeft_preservesLimits`) and the epi lemmas (`epi_whiskerRight_of_epi`) need only `RigidCategory`. The proof therefore goes through with the instance supplied by hand. The deleted hypothesis is a consequence of the others, since tensoring is additive in a rigid abelian category.
- `[Epi p₁]` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The theorem remains true for arbitrary p₁. Reduction to the epi case: (1) split p₁ = e ≫ m with e = factorThruImage p₁ epi and m = image.ι p₁ mono. (2) Then p₁ ⊗ₘ p₂ = (e ⊗ₘ p₂) ≫ (m ▷ W₂) by functoriality of ⊗ₘ. (3) m ▷ W₂ is mono because tensorRight W₂ is a right adjoint in a rigid category (TensorExact.lean: tensorRight_preservesLimits), so Mathlib's `kernelSubobject_comp_mono` gives kernelSubobject (p₁ ⊗ₘ p₂) = kernelSubobject (e ⊗ₘ p₂). (4) The present argument applied to the epi e bounds this by imageSubobject (kernel.ι e ▷ Z₂) ⊔ imageSubobject (Z₁ ◁ kernel.ι p₂). (5) Since m is mono, kernel.ι e = iso ≫ kernel.ι p₁, so kernel.ι e ▷ Z₂ = (iso ▷ Z₂) ≫ (kernel.ι p₁ ▷ Z₂); whiskering an iso gives an iso, so `imageSubobject_iso_comp` shows the two images are equal. That is exactly the original bound. Degenerate case p₁ = 0: kernel.ι p₁ is an iso, so imageSubobject (kernel.ι p₁ ▷ Z₂) = ⊤ and the claim is trivial. Rigidity is not removable (counterexample in ℤ-Mod with p₁ : ℤ → ℤ/2, p₂ = ·2), but the epi assumption is. This and [MonoidalPreadditive A] can each be removed on their own, and also together.

Write p₁ = e ≫ m with e = factorThruImage p₁ (an epi) and m = image.ι p₁ (a mono). Then p₁ ⊗ₘ p₂ = (e ⊗ₘ p₂) ≫ (m ▷ W₂) by functoriality of ⊗ₘ (`tensor_comp` with p₂ ≫ 𝟙). The map m ▷ W₂ is mono because `tensorRight W₂` preserves limits in a rigid category (`tensorRight_preservesLimits` in TensorExact.lean). Mathlib's `kernelSubobject_comp_mono` (Subobject/Limits.lean:253) then gives kernelSubobject (p₁ ⊗ₘ p₂) = kernelSubobject (e ⊗ₘ p₂). This is a subobject of the same object Z₁ ⊗ Z₂, so the theorem as stated (with Epi e) bounds it by imageSubobject (kernel.ι e ▷ Z₂) ⊔ imageSubobject (Z₁ ◁ kernel.ι p₂). Because m is mono, kernel.ι e = i ≫ kernel.ι p₁ for an iso i (`kernelCompMono`). So kernel.ι e ▷ Z₂ = (i ▷ Z₂) ≫ (kernel.ι p₁ ▷ Z₂), where i ▷ Z₂ is an iso. Mathlib's `imageSubobject_iso_comp` (Subobject/Limits.lean:459) shows the two images are equal, which gives exactly the original bound. This also matches the known fact that tensoring is exact in each variable in a rigid abelian category. The case p₁ = 0 is consistent with this.

If 3 ≤ p, run the existing induction unchanged; it only needs 2 ≤ p, for `half_pred_pow_succ` and `RoundUp.lowDigits_cons`. `hodd` cannot be dropped: for p = 4 and m = 2, (16 - 1) / 2 = 7 has base-4 digits [3, 1], and 3 > (4 - 1) / 2 = 1.

Case p = 1: 1^m - 1 = 0 and 0/2 = 0, so the goal is `LowDigits 1 0`. That follows from `RoundUp.lowDigits_zero (p : ℕ) : LowDigits p 0` (RoundUp.lean:86), which holds for every p. Case 3 ≤ p: the existing induction goes through unchanged. It only needs 2 ≤ p, for `half_pred_pow_succ` (SubtractBranch.lean:148, which takes `2 ≤ p` and `Odd p`) and for `RoundUp.lowDigits_cons` (RoundUp.lean:92, which takes `2 ≤ p`). Its digit bound `le_refl _` does not depend on p. So the theorem stays true and provable with `hp` deleted.

In fact the whole function is then `z ↦ a z.1 * (const + correction P (c z.1) 0)`, which is smooth by `ha'` and `hr`. In both cases the final `ha'.mul (hb.add hr)` gives the result. So the statement holds for every real `R`.

The step `hr` uses `correction_family_contDiffOn` on `U ×ˢ univ` and does not need `hR`. Without `hR`: if `R ≠ 0`, then `div_ne_zero hz.2.ne' hR0` gives `z.2 / R ≠ 0` and the same argument works. ### [`NavierStokes.PolarCharts.chart_finiteJets_uniform`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/PhysicalGraphBounds.lean#L336-L354) — `lean-pool-0108`: confirmed

- `(ha : 0 < a)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The proof uses `ha` only to get `ContDiff ℝ ∞ (chart a j)` from `chart_contDiff ha`. The rest of the proof needs only that each `iteratedFDeriv ℝ k (chart a j)` is continuous, so that it is bounded on the compact closed ball, and then takes a finite sum over `Index × Fin (m+1)`. But `chart a j` is smooth for every real `a`.

Here `chart a j p = ((extendedBase a (rotate j p)).1, (extendedBase a (rotate j p)).2 + offset j)`, where `extendedBase a p = (√(positiveRadius (a^2/32) (p.1^2+p.2^2)), arctan (p.2 / positiveRadius (a/8) p.1))` and `positiveRadius ℓ x = ℓ + (x-ℓ) * smoothTransition ((x-ℓ)/ℓ)`. `positiveRadius_contDiff` holds for every `ℓ`. The only places `extendedBase_contDiff` uses `0 < a` are the two non-vanishing side conditions (for `sqrt` and for the division).

- Case `a = 0`: `(x-0)/0 = 0` and `smoothTransition 0 = 0`, so `positiveRadius 0 x = 0` for every `x`. Then `extendedBase 0 p = (√0, arctan (p.2/0)) = (0, 0)`, so `chart 0 j` is constant and hence smooth.
- Case `a ≠ 0`: `a^2/32 > 0`, so `positiveRadius_pos` makes the `sqrt` argument positive. For `a > 0` the existing argument applies. For `a < 0`, `ℓ = a/8 < 0` and `positiveRadius ℓ x < 0` for all `x`. If `x ≥ ℓ`, then `(x-ℓ)/ℓ ≤ 0`, the transition is `0`, and the value is `ℓ < 0`. If `x < ℓ`, then `(x-ℓ) * smoothTransition(...) ≤ 0` because `smoothTransition ≥ 0`, so the value is `≤ ℓ < 0`. So the denominator never vanishes, and the same `ContDiff.sqrt` / `ContDiff.div` / `arctan` composition gives smoothness.

With `ContDiff ℝ ∞ (chart a j)` established for every `a`, the rest of the proof works unchanged: `continuous_iteratedFDeriv`, `IsCompact.exists_bound_of_continuousOn`, and the bound `1 + ∑ |B i|`. Degenerate cases: if `b < 0` the ball is empty and the statement is trivially true. The statement stays true and provable without `ha`.
  - verifier: I checked the definitions in PhysicalGraphBounds.lean (lines 273–303) and RadialPullback.lean (lines 1176–1202). `positiveRadius ℓ x = ℓ + (x - ℓ) * smoothTransition ((x - ℓ) / ℓ)`, and `positiveRadius_contDiff` holds for every `ℓ`. In `extendedBase_contDiff`, `ha` is used only to supply the two non-vanishing conditions, for `sqrt` and for the division. `chart_contDiff` uses `ha` only through `extendedBase_contDiff`.

**Case `a = 0`.** Since `x / 0 = 0` and `smoothTransition 0 = 0` (`zero_of_nonpos`), we get `positiveRadius 0 x = x * 0 = 0`. So `extendedBase 0 p = (√0, arctan (p.2 / 0)) = (0, 0)`. Then `chart 0 j` is constant, hence smooth.

**Case `a < 0`.** First, `a^2/32 > 0`, so `positiveRadius_pos` makes the `sqrt` argument positive. Next, `ℓ = a/8 < 0`:
- If `x ≥ ℓ`, then `(x - ℓ)/ℓ ≤ 0`, so the transition is 0 and the value is `ℓ < 0`.
- If `x < ℓ`, then `(x - ℓ) * smoothTransition(...) ≤ 0`, because `smoothTransition.nonneg`, so the value is `≤ ℓ < 0`.

So the denominator never vanishes, and the same `ContDiff.sqrt` / `ContDiff.div` / `arctan` composition gives `ContDiff ℝ ∞ (extendedBase a)`, and therefore `ContDiff ℝ ∞ (chart a j)`.

**Rest of the proof.** The remainder of `chart_finiteJets_uniform` (continuity of `iteratedFDeriv`, the bound on the compact closed ball, the finite sum) only needs this smoothness. `ha` is not mentioned anywhere else in the statement, and `a` remains bound as an implicit argument because it occurs in the conclusion. Deleting `ha` therefore leaves the theorem true and provable.

- If b = a, every point where γd is nonzero has first coordinate a. γd is continuous by `hγ`, so if γd(p) ≠ 0 then also γd(p.1+ε, p.2) ≠ 0 for small ε > 0. That point has first coordinate a + ε ≠ a, a contradiction. So γd = 0.

- `normalizeSource d a 0 z = sourceMultiplier .. • 0 = 0`.
- `compactIntegral χ M v 0 z = pastIntegral .. - χ z.1 • totalIntegral .. = ∫ 0 - χ • ∫ 0 = 0`; both are Bochner integrals of the zero function.
- `pullback d a 0 = 0 ∘ liftChart _ = 0`. So `physicalCompact d a b M v 0 = 0`, and `streamPotential = divideRadius 0 = 0 / p.1 = 0`.
- `streamBeta w 0 = -fderiv ℝ 0 p (0,w) = 0`. `streamGamma k v 0 = graphDr k v 0 + divideRadius 0 = 0` (using `fderiv_zero`).
- Hence `graphDivergence = fderiv 0 .. + 0/p.1 + fderiv 0 .. = 0`.

New proof: split on `a < b`. If it holds, use the existing proof. Otherwise show γd = 0 as in Step 1 (using `hs` and `hγ.continuous`), substitute it, and finish by unfolding the definitions and simplifying with `integral_zero`, `fderiv_zero` / `fderiv_const`, `smul_zero`, `zero_div`, `mul_zero`. The degenerate cases are b < a (empty support) and a = b (handled by continuity).

New proof: split on `a < b`. If it holds, use the current proof. If `b ≤ a`, then γd = 0. When b < a, `Icc a b` is empty, so the support is empty. When b = a, the support lies in {p | p.1 = a}. Since γd is continuous (from hγ), its support is open, and an open set inside the line p.1 = a must be empty. Then every term vanishes when unfolded with the definitions I checked in the sources:
- `weightedSource 0 = fun p => p.1 * 0`, which is 0.
- `normalizeSource d a 0 = sourceMultiplier • 0`, which is 0.
- `compactIntegral χ M v 0 = pastIntegral - χ • totalIntegral`, where both are Bochner integrals of 0, so it is 0 (`integral_zero`).
- `pullback d a 0 = 0 ∘ liftChart`, which is 0. So `physicalCompact` is 0, and `streamPotential = divideRadius 0 = 0 / p.1`, which is 0.

### [`polytopeRoundedSupportCurve_regular`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/OperatorTheory/Operator/Crouzeix/SmoothSupportCurve.lean#L231-L242) — `lean-pool-0133`: confirmed

- `(hu : u.Nonempty)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The statement still holds when `u = ∅`, because of how the empty sum and `Real.log 0` are defined. In that case `polytopeSoftPartition ∅ delta theta = ∑ z ∈ ∅, … = 0` (`Finset.sum_empty`). Then `Real.log 0 = 0` (`Real.log_zero`), so `polytopeSoftSupport ∅ delta theta = delta * 0 = 0`. Hence `polytopeRoundedSupport ∅ delta rho = fun _ => rho`, which can be shown with `funext` and `simp [polytopeRoundedSupport, polytopeSoftSupport, polytopeSoftPartition]`.

Proof without `hu`: case split on whether `u` is nonempty.
- If `u` is nonempty, use the existing proof unchanged.
- Otherwise `u = ∅` (`Finset.not_nonempty_iff_eq_empty`). Rewrite `polytopeRoundedSupport ∅ delta rho` to the constant function and apply `smoothSupportCurve_regular` with `contDiff_const`. The curvature hypothesis then reads `0 < rho + deriv (deriv fun _ => rho) theta`. The double derivative of a constant is 0 (`deriv_const'` twice), so this reduces to `0 < rho`, which is `hrho`.

Geometrically, the curve is then the circle `rho • smoothSupportUnitNormal`, whose velocity `rho • smoothSupportUnitTangent` is never zero.

The other two hypotheses are needed:
- `hrho`: with `rho = 0` and `u = {z}`, the support function is linear in cos and sin, so `h + h'' = 0` and the derivative vanishes.
- `hdelta`: a negative `delta` gives a soft-min whose `h + h''` changes sign, so it has a zero.
  - verifier: I checked the definitions. `polytopeSoftPartition u delta theta` is `∑ z ∈ u, Real.exp (...)`, so it is 0 when u = ∅. `polytopeSoftSupport u delta theta` is `delta * Real.log (partition)`, which is then `delta * Real.log 0 = 0`. `polytopeRoundedSupport u delta rho theta` is `softSupport + rho`, so for u = ∅ it is the constant function `fun _ => rho`. The theorem `smoothSupportCurve_regular` (line 149 of the same file) takes only `ContDiff ℝ ∞ h` and `∀ theta, 0 < smoothSupportCurvatureRadius h theta`. The curvature radius is defined as `h theta + deriv (deriv h) theta`. For the constant function, `contDiff_const` gives smoothness, and the curvature radius is `rho + deriv (deriv (fun _ => rho)) theta = rho + 0 = rho > 0` by `hrho`. When u is nonempty, the existing proof works unchanged. `hu` appears nowhere else in the statement, so the theorem still elaborates without it. The case split on `u.Nonempty`, with `Finset.not_nonempty_iff_eq_empty` for the empty case, therefore gives a proof without `hu`.

### [`NavierStokes.ActivationCone.natural_reference_bounds`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/ActivationCone.lean#L819-L863) — `lean-pool-0138`: confirmed

- `(hδlim : 2 * δ < ReferencePath.rampLimit)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: The conclusion only involves `A = referenceP1 (refLog N δ)` and `B = referenceP2 N.endpoint (refLog N δ) (refAxial N δ)` on `Icc 0 τ ×ˢ Icc (-1) 1`, for some `τ ≤ δ` that we get to choose. Both are built from `radialPartial = fderiv ℝ · p (1,0)`, so they only depend on how `refLog N δ` and `refAxial N δ` behave near each point. `ReferencePath.continuation δ G p = G (0,p.2) + ∫ x in 0..p.1, slopeCutoff δ x * radialPartial G (x,p.2)`. `slopeCutoff_one` says `slopeCutoff δ x = 1` for `x ≤ δ` (it only needs `0 < δ`). So if `δ' ≤ δ` with `0 < δ'`, the integrands of `continuation δ G` and `continuation δ' G` agree pointwise on `uIcc 0 p.1` whenever `p.1 ≤ δ'`. Hence `continuation δ G = continuation δ' G` on the open set `Iio δ' ×ˢ univ`, with no smoothness needed. Proof without `hδlim`: set `δ' := min δ (rampLimit / 4)`, so `0 < δ'`, `2δ' < rampLimit` and `δ' ≤ δ`. Apply the theorem itself (in its current form, which keeps `hδlim`) to `δ'`. This gives `τ, α, M` with `τ ≤ δ'`. Take `τ/2` in place of `τ`: then `0 < τ/2 ≤ δ`, and every `y ∈ Icc 0 (τ/2)` satisfies `y < δ'`. Near such points `refLog N δ` agrees with `refLog N δ'` and `refAxial N δ` with `refAxial N δ'`, so their `fderiv`s are equal there. `N.endpoint` does not depend on `δ`. So `A` and `B` for `δ` equal those for `δ'` on the shrunken box, and all the bounds carry over. Degenerate cases: if `δ` is large, `refLog N δ` may behave badly far from 0, but that region never enters the argument. `hδ` itself cannot be removed, because `0 < τ ≤ δ` forces `δ > 0`.
  - verifier: I checked the definitions in the sources, and the reduction to a smaller δ' works.

**Definitions.**
- `ReferencePath.continuation δ G p = G (0, p.2) + primitive (dampedSlope δ G) p`.
- `ProfileHistories.primitive F p = ∫ x in 0..p.1, F (x, p.2)`.
- `dampedSlope δ G p = slopeCutoff δ p.1 * radialPartial G p`.
- `ProfileHistories.radialPartial F p = fderiv ℝ F p (1, 0)`, which depends only on the germ of `F` at `p`.
- `referenceP1 L p = -2 * radialPartial L p`.
- `referenceP2 X0 L U p = -2 * radialPartial U p / (√(2 * X0 * exp p.1) * exp (L p))`. This uses the germ of `U` at `p` and the value `L p`.
- `N` and `N.endpoint` do not depend on δ.

**Agreement for different cutoffs.** Let `0 < δ' ≤ δ`. `slopeCutoff_one` only needs `0 < δ`. For any `p` with `p.1 ≤ δ'`, every `x` in `uIcc 0 p.1` satisfies `x ≤ max 0 p.1 ≤ δ'`; this covers negative `p.1` too. So both cutoffs equal 1 there, and `intervalIntegral.integral_congr` makes the two integrals equal, with no integrability needed. Hence `continuation δ G` and `continuation δ' G` agree on the open set `{p | p.1 < δ'}`. Their `fderiv`s at such points are therefore equal, and so are their values.

**Proof without `hδlim`.** Take `δ' := min δ (rampLimit / 4)`.
- `rampLimit = log (41/40) > 0`, so `δ' > 0` and `2δ' ≤ rampLimit / 2 < rampLimit`.
- Apply the original theorem at `δ'` to get `τ, α, M` with `0 < τ ≤ δ'`.
- Use `τ/2` instead. Then `0 < τ/2 ≤ δ`, and every `y ∈ Icc 0 (τ/2)` satisfies `y < τ ≤ δ'`.
- On that box, `A` and `B` for δ coincide with `A` and `B` for δ'.
- `Icc 0 (τ/2) ⊆ Icc 0 τ`, so all the bounds transfer.

**Statement without it.** `hδlim` is not mentioned in the conclusion or in any other binder, so deleting it is a pure deletion and the statement still elaborates. Only the proof changes.

The other factors do not need it. `physicalHeat_contDiffAt` needs `1 < 1 + h`, which follows from `hh`. The square-root denominator needs only `hs`. Nothing in the statement mentions `hh1`.

### [`NavierStokes.TemporalMeanUpdate.meanClass_axialAlias`](https://github.com/Vilin97/lean-pool/blob/01db1d7049cf9668bda1e9e106875ee1278b43e6/LeanPool/NavierStokesAndEuler/NavierStokes/TemporalMeanUpdate.lean#L1535-L1567) — `lean-pool-0159`: confirmed

- `(hM : ∀ n, M n ≠ 0)` — **confirmed**
  - judge: **Where `hM` is used.** The proof passes `hM` to `meanClass_dividedAlias`. That lemma passes it to `dividedAlias_global_bounds`. There its only use is the call `hbA := UniformFourierAlias.real_exactAlias_finiteJets .radial … m 0`, which needs `M n ≠ 0`. That call is made with Fourier exponent `p = 0`, so the bound it delivers is just `KA * B`: it gets no gain from oscillation and needs no information about M. The other steps are stated for arbitrary `M`: `physicalAlias_contDiff`, `physicalAlias_supported`, `dividedAlias_interior_support` (∀ M), `dividedAlias_finiteJets_transfer` (`hbT` takes any `M`) and `meanClass_of_interior_bounds`.

**A bound that holds for every M, including 0.** `exactAlias χ M v G = deriv χ z.1 • totalIntegral M v G z`, and `totalIntegral M v G z = ∫ u, G (z + (u, (M*u)•v))`. The source bound `hsource` from `meanClass_global_finiteJets` holds for all `k ≤ m` and all `x` (the proof uses it with `Set.mem_univ x`).
- `TransportPrimitive.iteratedFDeriv_totalIntegral_norm_le` holds for any `M`. It gives `‖D^i totalIntegral M v (G n) y‖ ≤ B * (b^d - a^d)`, using that `G n` is supported radially in `[a^d, b^d]` (`normalizeSource_supported`).
- `RadialPullback.radial_multiplier_finiteJets_uniform` with `h = deriv χ` (smooth) multiplies this by a constant `K`.
- Outside `Icc (a^d) (b^d)` the derivatives vanish by `exactAlias_supported` and `iteratedFDeriv_supported`.

Together these give `‖D^i exactAlias χ (M n) v (G n) y‖ ≤ K (b^d - a^d) B` for every `M`, including `M = 0`. This is exactly the argument of the library lemma `exactAlias_bounded_fiber` in `LocalSignedRequest.lean`, which is stated for all `M` with no nonzero condition. That file comes later in the import chain, so here the short argument would be repeated inline from the same lemmas, which are available to this file. Replacing `KA` by this `K'` in `dividedAlias_global_bounds` proves it, and hence `meanClass_dividedAlias` and this theorem, without `hM`.

**Degenerate case.** At `M n = 0`, `totalIntegral` is just the radial integral of a compactly supported smooth source. It is smooth and bounded by the estimate above, so nothing breaks. `M` stays an argument of the theorem because it appears in the conclusion; only `hM` is removed.

That lemma requires `M ≠ 0`, and with p = 0 its bound reduces to `KA * B` with no dependence on M. All the other ingredients hold for every M: `dividedAlias_finiteJets_transfer` (line 1010, `∀ M v g`), `dividedAlias_interior_support` (`∀ M`), `physicalAlias_contDiff`/`physicalAlias_supported` (M is an explicit argument with no hypothesis), and `meanClass_of_interior_bounds`. It needs the source to be smooth and radially supported in [a^d, b^d] (`normalizeSource_supported`), plus a bound on that slab. `hsource` gives that bound: `meanClass_global_finiteJets` returns a `FiniteJetBound m (G n) Set.univ B`. The result is ‖D^i totalIntegral‖ ≤ B(b^d − a^d). `RadialPullback.radial_multiplier_finiteJets_uniform`, applied with the smooth `deriv χ`, turns this into a bound K·B(b^d − a^d) on the slab z.1 ∈ Icc. Off the slab the derivatives vanish, by `exactAlias_supported` and `iteratedFDeriv_supported`. This is exactly the proof of the library's own `exactAlias_bounded_fiber` (LocalSignedRequest.lean:1445), which is stated for all M. The library's later `LocalSignedRequest.meanClass_dividedAlias` is documented as 'No mean-zero condition or nonzero transport coefficient is needed' and takes arbitrary M. Feeding this bound into `hbT` proves `dividedAlias_global_bounds` without `hM`, and hence this theorem. At M n = 0 nothing breaks: `totalIntegral` is just the radial integral of the compactly supported source.

By definition `loss n = ((n:ℝ)^2)⁻¹`. For n = 0 this is `0⁻¹ = 0 < 1`. For n ≥ 2 it is at most 1/4 < 1. So n = 1. But `splitM K 1 = ⌈K * Real.sqrt ((1:ℝ) * Real.log 1)⌉₊`, and `Real.log 1 = 0`, so this is `⌈0⌉₊ = 0 < 1`. That contradicts the assumption. The theorem can therefore derive `hsmall` at the start (the case split on n is short, using `Real.log_one`, `Real.sqrt_zero` and `Nat.ceil_zero`) and then run the existing proof unchanged. That proof uses `hsmall` only for `hsplit` and `recursiveCostBound_step hn hsmall`. The other hypotheses are needed. Without `hn`, if n < n0 the left side is the base value 2^n·2^(n/5), which beats the right side, e.g. with K = 0 and n large. Without `hslack`, the ceiling in `costMultiplier` can exceed (1+2·loss n)(4/3)^a, e.g. when splitA = 1 and n is large.

No other `loss` is in scope that could shadow this one. Suppose `¬ splitM K n < n`. Then truncated subtraction gives `splitA K n = 0`, so `hslack` becomes `1 ≤ loss n * 1 = (n^2)⁻¹`. If n = 0, the right side is `0⁻¹ = 0`, so this fails. If n ≥ 2, the right side is at most 1/4, so it also fails. That leaves n = 1. But then `splitM K 1 = ⌈K * √(1 * log 1)⌉₊ = ⌈K * √0⌉₊ = ⌈0⌉₊ = 0 < 1`, which contradicts the assumption. So `hsmall` follows from `hslack`. ### [`P2MW.S_exists_isIdempotentElem_mul_iterate_eq_zero_sum_iterate_eq_one_of_not_isField.C2idemCore.IdempotentOrbit.exists_idempotent_orbit_or_isField`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_exists_isIdempotentElem_mul_iterate_eq_zero_sum_iterate_eq_one_of_not_isField.lean#L172-L278) — `flt-anthropic-0014`: confirmed

Let t = conj φ s; t^n = id with n ≥ 1, so t is a ring automorphism of P = Π i, F i. Each F i is a field, so the idempotents of P are exactly the {0,1}-valued functions, i.e. indicators of arbitrary subsets of ι, even when ι is infinite. Both t and t⁻¹ preserve the order e ≤ e' ↔ e*e' = e, so t sends the minimal nonzero idempotents ind {i} to each other: t (ind {i}) = ind {ψ i} for a permutation ψ. From t^n = id and injectivity of ind on singletons, ψ^[n] = id, so the orbit O = (range n).image (ψ^[·] j₀) is a finite set mapped onto itself by ψ. Since ind O is a finite sum of singleton indicators, t (ind O) = ind (ψ '' O) = ind O. So a = φ.symm (ind O) is an idempotent fixed by s. By hfix, a = f x; since f is injective, x is an idempotent of the field k, hence 0 or 1, so ind O is 0 or 1. As j₀ ∈ O, ind O ≠ 0, so ind O = 1, which gives O = univ. Hence ι is finite. Then Fintype.ofFinite supplies the [Fintype ι] that the helper exists_indexMap_of_iterate_eq needs, and the existing proof applies unchanged. The statement is therefore still true and provable without [Finite ι].
  - verifier: I checked the reviewer's argument and it holds. Removing `[Finite ι]` does not affect elaboration: no other binder or the conclusion mentions it, and the sum in the conclusion is over `Finset.range n` in R.

**Empty ι.** R is a subsingleton, so e = 0 gives the first disjunct, exactly as in the existing proof.

**Nonempty ι, with j₀ ∈ ι.**
- R is nontrivial. So f, a ring hom from the field k, is injective.
- Since s^[n] = id, s is bijective. Hence t = conj φ s is a ring automorphism of P = Π i, F i.
- Each F i is a field, so the idempotents of P are exactly the {0,1}-valued functions, i.e. the indicators of arbitrary subsets of ι. Their order (e ≤ e' iff e·e' = e) is that of the power set of ι, and this holds for infinite ι too.
- t and t⁻¹ preserve idempotents and this order, so t permutes the atoms ind {i}. This gives a permutation ψ with t (ind {i}) = ind {ψ i}.
- From t^[n] = id and injectivity of ind on singletons, ψ^[n] = id. So the orbit O of j₀ is a finite, ψ-invariant set.
- ind O is a finite sum of distinct singleton indicators, so t (ind O) = ind O. Thus φ.symm (ind O) is an idempotent fixed by s.
- By hfix and injectivity of f, it is f x for an idempotent x of k, so x = 0 or x = 1.
- ind O ≠ 0 because j₀ ∈ O and F j₀ is nontrivial. So ind O = 1, which forces O = univ, and ι is finite.

After that, `Fintype.ofFinite` gives the `Fintype` instance the helper lemma needs, and the existing proof goes through. I found no counterexample, and the degenerate cases (empty ι, n = 1) are covered.

Second, orderOf σ = d holds without primality. hθ makes L_w/K_v Galois with group ⟨θ⟩, and hw (via `isField_V`) makes it unramified. Then O_w = O_v[α] with α lifting a generator of the residue extension, and the minimal polynomial of α has separable reduction (finite fields). So θ^k(α) ≡ α mod ϖ forces θ^k(α) = α, i.e. θ^k = id. Hence σ^k ≠ 1 for 0 < k < d, and σ^d = 1 by `sigmaV_pow_finrank`. With orderOf σ = d, the rest of `finrank_ker_le_one` (fixed field of ⟨σ⟩ is k_v, Hilbert-90 style kernel bound) and the rest of the proof go through unchanged. Degenerate cases: hres forces θ ≠ id, so d ≥ 2; and in the d = 1 case the formula q^{min(s,m)} can be checked directly. Each of hres and hprime is claimed deletable on its own (the other kept); deleting both together was not checked.
  - verifier: hprime has two uses, which I checked in the source. (1) `hprime.pos`, both in `main` (for `v_c_eq_one`) and in `finrank_ker_le_one`. Here d > 0 holds anyway: L_w is a nonzero finite-dimensional K_v-space (`finrank_int` already uses a finite free basis). (2) `orderOf_sigmaV`, which uses `orderOf_eq_prime`. `fO_injective` and `associated_det_fO` do not take hprime. Without primality, orderOf σ = d still holds once hres is kept. `sigmaV_pow_finrank` gives σ^d = 1. Conversely, suppose σ^k = 1 for some 0 < k < d. Then θ^k acts trivially on k_w; this uses hw, which makes the extension unramified (`map_maximalIdeal`) with V ≅ k_w of degree d. The Hensel argument then forces θ^k = id: take α lifting a generator of k_w; then L_w = K_v(α), f̄ is separable and ᾱ is a simple root, so θ^k(α) = α. But θ^k = id contradicts orderOf θ = d. Hence orderOf σ = d, and the fixed-field computation and the rest of `finrank_ker_le_one` and `main` go through unchanged. The d = 1 case is vacuous, since then θ = id and hres would need ‖0‖ = 1.

### [`P2MW.S_ModularCurve_contDiff_and_finsum_smoothedFundamental_eq_one.ModularCurve.SmoothedFundamentalProof.finite_support_smul`](https://github.com/anthropics/fermats-last-theorem/blob/6e837e75355538c7f80bab5b956861e86c4eacc2/P2M/Sol/S_ModularCurve_contDiff_and_finsum_smoothedFundamental_eq_one.lean#L435-L442) — `flt-anthropic-0144`: confirmed

- `[Γ.FiniteIndex]` — **confirmed**
  - judge: `ModularCurve.smoothedFundamental Γ T z` is a `finsum` over `SL(2,ℤ) ⧸ Γ` (Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_SmoothedFundamental.lean, line 170). That definition needs no finiteness; with infinite index the finsum may take the junk value 0, which only shrinks the support. The current proof uses FiniteIndex only through `carrier`/`isCompact_carrierH`/`mem_carrier_of_ne_zero`, i.e. through the Fintype instance on the quotient. Alternative proof without it: let S = {δ : SL(2,ℤ) | puCut T (mob δ τ) ≠ 0}, which is finite by `hasFiniteSupport_puCut_mob T τ` (no Γ, no FiniteIndex). If smoothedFundamental Γ T (γ•τ) ≠ 0, then by the contrapositive of `finsum_eq_zero_of_forall_eq_zero` (additive form of `finprod_eq_one_of_forall_eq_one`) some coset q has puCut T (mob q.out (γ•τ)) ≠ 0. By `coe_smul` and `mob_mob` this equals puCut T (mob (q.out * γ) τ), so δ := q.out*γ ∈ S. Since γ ∈ Γ, (q.out)⁻¹δ = γ ∈ Γ, so ⟦δ⟧ = q (left cosets) and γ = (⟦δ⟧.out)⁻¹ * δ. Hence the support lies in the image of the finite set S under δ ↦ (⟦δ⟧.out)⁻¹δ and is finite. Degenerate cases: infinite index only shrinks the support; when Γ is trivial the support still injects into S.
  - verifier: 1. It is an argument: `[Γ.FiniteIndex]` is instance-implicit, it mentions only Γ, and Γ is an argument (it appears in the statement), so the theorem takes it.

2. The statement elaborates without it. `ModularCurve.smoothedFundamental` (Definitions/Def_ModularCurve_SmoothedFundamental.lean:170) is a `finsum` over `SL(2,ℤ) ⧸ Γ` and needs no instance. `Function.support`, the subgroup coercion, the action on ℍ and `Set.Finite` don't need it either. Only the proof uses FiniteIndex, through `carrier`, `isCompact_carrierH` and `mem_carrier_of_ne_zero`, which rely on the scoped `Fintype (SL(2,ℤ) ⧸ Γ)` instance.

3. The statement stays true and provable without it.
- `hasFiniteSupport_puCut_mob T τ` (line 197) shows that S = {δ | puCut T (mob δ τ) ≠ 0} is finite. Neither it nor `hasFiniteSupport_bump_mob` / `finite_moving` mentions Γ or FiniteIndex.
- Suppose smoothedFundamental Γ T (γ•τ) ≠ 0. The finsum is then nonzero, so some term is nonzero: by the contrapositive of `finsum_eq_zero_of_forall_eq_zero`, which holds whether or not the support is finite, there is a coset q with puCut T (mob q.out ↑(γ•τ)) ≠ 0.
- By `coe_smul` and `mob_mob` (both in the definitions file), that term equals puCut T (mob (q.out*γ) τ), so δ := q.out*γ lies in S.
- Because γ ∈ Γ, ⟦δ⟧ = q (`QuotientGroup.mk_mul_of_mem` plus `out_eq'`, the same step as in `cosetEquiv`). Hence γ = (⟦δ⟧.out)⁻¹ * δ.
- So the support, viewed in SL(2,ℤ), lies in the image of the finite set S under δ ↦ (⟦δ⟧.out)⁻¹δ. It is therefore finite, and since the inclusion of Γ is injective, the support in Γ is finite too.

With infinite index the finsum can only take the junk value 0, which removes elements from the support. No counterexample exists, and the argument has no gap.

  - judge: The statement stays true without `[IsIntegral Y]`. The only place the proof uses it is to show that φ = h.app V : A := Γ(Y,V) → B := Γ(X,h⁻¹V) is injective (through `basicOpen_eq_bot_iff`, which needs Y reduced), and to treat A as a domain in `height_comap_eq`.

Without the hypothesis, an affine-local argument works. Let I = ker φ. Since h is surjective, the induced map Spec B → Spec A is surjective, so every prime of A contains I. Hence I ⊆ nilradical(A); in fact the earlier basic-open argument shows directly that ker φ consists of nilpotents. Because B is a domain (X is integral and h⁻¹V is a nonempty affine open), A/I embeds into B, so A/I is a domain. Also:
- A/I is finitely generated over K, as a quotient of A, which is finite type through fY (`exists_algebra_finiteType`, which does not need integrality).
- B is integral over A/I.

So `height_comap_eq K` applies to the injective integral map A/I → B. It gives ht(p/I) = ht(q), where q = px and p = py = comap q. Since I lies in every prime of A, the primes of A below p correspond to the primes of A/I below p/I, so ht p = ht(p/I). The two `IsLocalization.AtPrime ... ringKrullDim_eq_height` rewrites need neither integrality nor reducedness of Y, so they still finish the proof.

Degenerate cases: Y is nonempty because it contains h.base x. Y is irreducible as the image of the irreducible X under the surjection h, so nothing else is lost.

Geometrically, h factors through Y_red, and the local rings of Y and Y_red differ only by nilpotents, which leaves the Krull dimension unchanged.
  - verifier: Deleting the binder leaves the statement well-formed: `[IsIntegral Y]` appears nowhere else in it, and the conclusion only needs the stalks and `h.base`. The current proof uses it in exactly two places: the injectivity of φ (through `basicOpen_eq_bot_iff`, which needs Y reduced) and the `[IsDomain A]` instance that `height_comap_eq` requires. I read `height_comap_eq` (line 196). It needs a field k, domains A and B, `Algebra k` instances with `FiniteType` on both rings, and an injective integral ring hom between them. It does not ask the hom to be k-linear, so separate K-algebra structures are enough.

The repair works. Write A = Γ(Y,V), B = Γ(X,h⁻¹V) and I = ker φ.
- B is a domain, because X is integral and h⁻¹V is nonempty (it contains x). So A/I embeds in B and is a domain.
- A/I is of finite type over K, as a quotient of A. `exists_algebra_finiteType` (line 233) only needs `LocallyOfFiniteType fY`, not integrality.
- B is integral over A/I, because it is integral over A.

So `height_comap_eq` applies to the injective integral map A/I → B and gives ht(py/I) = ht(px), where py = comap px. Since h is surjective, Spec B → Spec A is surjective, so every prime of A contains I. Hence I is contained in the nilradical, the primes of A below py correspond to the primes of A/I below py/I, and ht(py) = ht(py/I). The two `IsLocalization.AtPrime.ringKrullDim_eq_height` rewrites do not need integrality, so they still finish the proof.

Geometrically, h factors through Y_red, which is integral since Y is irreducible as the image of X; X → Y_red is still finite and surjective, and the stalks of Y and Y_red differ by a nil ideal, which does not change Krull dimension. I found no counterexample: Y is nonempty, and the open V contains h(x).

Suppose t is not transcendental, so it is algebraic over κ (the Fact t ≠ 0 is omitted, so t = 0 is allowed). (1) If t ≠ 0: t is algebraic, hence integral, over the field κ. By `IsIntegral.tower_top` it is integral over adjoin κ {t⁻¹}, so t ∈ chartRing κ {t⁻¹}. Then tInvChart κ t * ⟨t,_⟩ = 1, so tInvChart is a unit. Having it in the prime ideal 𝔮 contradicts IsPrime.ne_top, so `h` is false and the statement is vacuous. (2) If t = 0: t⁻¹ = 0 and tInvChart = 0. By hchartInv every element of the ring is aeval 0 f = algebraMap κ L (f.coeff 0), so every nonzero element is a unit and the prime 𝔮 must be ⊥ = Ideal.span {0}. The conclusion holds. So the new proof is: if t is transcendental, use the existing argument (eq_span_of_pow_mem with transcendental_inv); otherwise use the argument above. hchartInv cannot be dropped: with L = κ(u) and t = u⁻², chartRing κ {u²} = κ[u] and the prime (u) ∋ u² is not span{u²}.

2. Definitions checked. In `Definitions/Def_JacJ1_ChartAlgebra.lean`, `chartRing K S` is the subalgebra `{x | IsIntegral (Algebra.adjoin K S) x}` of the field L. It is therefore a domain, and an element of it whose inverse in L also lies in it is a unit. In `Def_AlgebraicCurve_CurveModelConstruction.lean`, `tInvChart K t` is `⟨t⁻¹, _⟩`. Because `[Fact (t ≠ 0)]` is omitted, t = 0 is allowed.

3. Without `ht`, if t is not transcendental, then t is algebraic over κ (`Transcendental` is defined as `¬IsAlgebraic`).

(a) Case t ≠ 0. Since κ is a field, t is integral over κ (`IsAlgebraic.isIntegral`). By `IsIntegral.tower_top`, using the scalar tower κ → adjoin κ {t⁻¹} → L, t is integral over adjoin κ {t⁻¹}, so t ∈ chartRing κ {t⁻¹}. Then `tInvChart κ t * ⟨t,_⟩ = 1`, so `tInvChart κ t` is a unit. A prime ideal cannot contain a unit (`IsPrime.ne_top`), so `h` is impossible and the statement holds vacuously.

(b) Case t = 0. Then t⁻¹ = 0 and `tInvChart κ t = 0`. By `hchartInv`, every element of the ring has the form `aeval 0 f = algebraMap κ L (f.coeff 0)`, so the ring is the image of the field κ. (Even without `hchartInv`: a domain that is integral over a field is a field.) The only prime ideal of a field is ⊥, and ⊥ = `Ideal.span {0}`, so the conclusion holds.

(c) If t is transcendental, the existing proof (`eq_span_of_pow_mem` with `transcendental_inv`) applies unchanged.

I found no gap or counterexample, so the theorem stays true and provable without `ht`.

### [`MvPowerSeries.isUnit_adicallyRestricted_iff_coeff_radical`](https://github.com/FormalFrontier/formal-power-series/blob/9bb57357300e4fad20d074ee4cd95e8317a34c81/FormalPowerSeries/MvPowerSeries/IdealAdicRestricted/UnitDetection.lean#L83-L92) — `formal-frontier-0162`: confirmed

- `[IsAdicComplete I R]` — **confirmed**

The existing proof needs it twice: in `isUnit_adicallyRestricted_iff_adicReduction_one` (through the geometric-series inverse) and in `isUnit_iff_quotient_of_isAdicComplete`. With the RHS as stated (a unit constant coefficient in `R` itself, and the other coefficients in `I.radical`), completeness is not needed.

The definition `adicallyRestrictedSubring I` (IdealAdicRestricted.lean:78) says: for every k, the image of the series in (R/I^k)[[σ]] is a polynomial. It does not involve completeness.

(→) If f is a unit in the subring, then `coeff 0 = constantCoeff ∘ Subring.subtype` is a ring hom, so `coeff 0 f` is a unit. Also `adicReduction I 1 f` is a unit in `MvPolynomial σ (R ⧸ I^1)`. By Mathlib's `MvPolynomial.isUnit_iff` (Algebra/MvPolynomial/Nilpotent.lean:58) together with `coeff_adicReduction` and `I^1 = I`, each `mk I (coeff α f)` with α ≠ 0 is nilpotent, i.e. `coeff α f ∈ I.radical`. This is exactly the first half of the existing proof of `..._coeff_mod_ideal`, and it does not use completeness.

(←) Since `coeff 0 f` is a unit, `MvPowerSeries.isUnit_iff_constantCoeff` gives an inverse g of f in `MvPowerSeries σ R`. It remains to show g is restricted. Fix k. The image of f in (R/I^k)[[σ]] is the coercion of a polynomial P_k. Its constant coefficient is a unit. Every other coefficient is the image of some r ∈ I.radical: if r^m ∈ I then r^{mk} ∈ I^k, so that image is nilpotent in R/I^k. By `MvPolynomial.isUnit_iff`, P_k has an inverse Q_k in (R/I^k)[σ]. Inverses are unique in (R/I^k)[[σ]], so `map (mk (I^k)) g = ↑Q_k`, a polynomial. Hence g lies in the subring, and f is a unit there.

Degenerate cases are fine. If I = ⊤, every series is restricted and I.radical = ⊤, so both sides reduce to `coeff 0 f` being a unit. A trivial R and an empty σ are also harmless. So the theorem stays true and provable after `omit [IsAdicComplete I R] in`.
  - verifier: 1. It is an argument. It is an instance-implicit variable, and `I` and `R` are both arguments, so the theorem takes it. No `omit` is in scope.

2. The statement elaborates without it. `adicallyRestrictedSubring` (IdealAdicRestricted.lean:78) is an infimum over k of the preimages under `map (mk (I^k))` of the polynomial range. It needs only `CommRing R` and `I`. Likewise `coeff`, `IsUnit` and `Ideal.radical` do not need completeness.

3. The statement is still true without it, and the proof sketch holds up.

(→) A unit in the subring stays a unit under the ring hom `constantCoeff ∘ subtype`, so `coeff 0 f` is a unit. Also `adicReduction I 1 f` is a unit. Mathlib's `MvPolynomial.isUnit_iff` (Nilpotent.lean:58) needs only `[CommRing R]` and works for any σ. With `coeff_adicReduction` and `I^1 = I` it makes `mk I (coeff α f)` nilpotent for α ≠ 0, i.e. `coeff α f ∈ I.radical`. This is the forward half of `..._coeff_mod_ideal`, which uses only the trivial direction of `..._adicReduction_one` (`hf.map`).

(←) Since the constant coefficient is a unit, f has an inverse g in `MvPowerSeries σ R`. Fix k. Then `map (mk (I^k)) f` is `↑P_k`. The constant coefficient of P_k is the image of a unit, hence a unit. For α ≠ 0, the coefficient is `mk (I^k) r` with `r^m ∈ I`; then `r^(mk) = (r^m)^k ∈ I^k`, so it is nilpotent. By `MvPolynomial.isUnit_iff`, P_k has an inverse Q_k. The coercion is a ring hom, so `↑Q_k` is an inverse of `map f` in the commutative ring `(R/I^k)[[σ]]`. So is `map g`, because `map` is a ring hom. Inverses are unique, so `map (mk (I^k)) g = ↑Q_k`, which lies in the polynomial range. So g is in the subring, and `f * g = 1` there.

The degenerate cases are fine: for k = 0 the quotient is trivial, and I = ⊤ is consistent. Neither direction uses completeness, so the hypothesis can be removed with `omit [IsAdicComplete I R] in` and a new proof.

Let A be the path component of p in U and A' the path component of p in V. Since U ∩ V is path-connected (hI) and contains p, we have U ∩ V ⊆ A ∩ A', and therefore A ∩ A' = U ∩ V.

(1) The path component PC of p in X lies in A ∪ A'. Take a path γ from p. By hcover and a Lebesgue-number argument, γ splits into finitely many pieces, each lying in U or in V. Every junction between a U-piece and a V-piece lies in U ∩ V ⊆ A ∩ A'. By induction, each U-piece starts in A and so stays in A, and each V-piece stays in A'.

(2) From (1), U ∩ PC = A: a point of U ∩ PC lies in A or in A' ⊆ V, and in the second case it lies in U ∩ V ⊆ A. Likewise V ∩ PC = A'. So A and A' are open in PC and cover it, both are path-connected, and their intersection U ∩ V is path-connected.

(3) Every loop at p, and every homotopy of such loops, has path-connected image containing p. So the inclusions A ↪ U, A' ↪ V and PC ↪ X induce isomorphisms of fundamental groups at p.

Now apply the theorem itself, with all its hypotheses, to the space PC and the cover A, A'. Pull φU and φV back along the isomorphisms π₁(A,p) ≅ π₁(U,p) and π₁(A',p) ≅ π₁(V,p). The compatibility condition carries over, because the map U ∩ V → U factors through A. The theorem then gives a unique φ' : π₁(PC,p) →* G. Composing φ' with the inverse of π₁(PC,p) ≅ π₁(X,p) gives the required φ. Existence and uniqueness follow because the isomorphisms in (3) are bijective and every square commutes.

The degenerate cases are covered: `p` makes U ∩ V nonempty. Hence hU can be dropped. The same argument applies to hV, and in fact both can be dropped together.
